According to a report by the Syrian Network for Human Rights, critics and opponents of the HTS were subject to repression in forms of enforced disappearances and tortures.[^cite_note-174] Shortly after the fall of the Assad regime, [Israel commenced a ground invasion](78549398.md) of the [Purple Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purple_Line_\(ceasefire_line\)) buffer zone near the Golan Heights, as well as commencing a series of airstrikes against Syrian military depots and naval bases.[^cite_note-NYT_12-8-175][^cite_note-176] The [Israeli Defense Forces](39237.md) claims that it is destroying Ba'athist military infrastructure, including chemical weapons plants, so that the rebels cannot use them.[^cite_note-NYT_12-8-175]&#x20;

Despite the collapse of the Assad regime, Turkish-backed [Syrian National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_National_Army) fighters in northern Syria continued their [offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manbij_offensive_\(2024\)) against U.S.-backed [Syrian Democratic Forces](48205248.md) (SDF) forces until a ceasefire was reached on 11 December.[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-178] In February 2025, the SDF, the [Autonomous Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Autonomous_Administration_of_North_and_East_Syria), and the [Syrian Democratic Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Democratic_Council) decided in a meeting that SDF would merge with the [Syrian Armed Forces](27479.md). The [International Coalition against ISIS](44120613.md) voiced support for continued dialogue between the SDF and the new Syrian government as well.[^cite_note-179]&#x20;

The prime minister of the transitional government, Mohammed al-Bashir, promised that Christians and other minorities would be permitted to continue practicing their religion without interference. However, this assurance was met with doubts, as many rebel forces had previous connections to [al-Qaeda](1921.md) and the [Islamic State](9087364.md).[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182] The use of a variation of the [*Tawhid* flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_flag) by the new government alongside [the opposition flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Syria) also raised worries, as it implied the new state may be [less secular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_and_secularism).[^cite_note-flag1-183][^cite_note-flag2-184] [Aisha al-Dibs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aisha_al-Dibs) was appointed as the Minister of Women's Affairs on 22 December 2024.[^cite_note-185] On 12 December 2024, a spokesman of the transitional government speaking to [Agence France-Presse](471981.md) said that during the government's three-month term, the [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Syria) and [parliament](4504009.md) would be suspended and that a 'judicial and human rights committee' would be established to review the constitution, prior to making amendments.[^cite_note-186]&#x20;



Syrian President [Ahmed al-Sharaa](44007699.md) with U.S. President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and Saudi Crown Prince [Mohammed bin Salman](38689358.md) in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia, 14 May 2025

On 29 January 2025, during the [Syrian Revolution Victory Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Revolution_Victory_Conference) in [Presidential Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Palace_of_Syria), the Syrian General Command appointed [Ahmed al-Sharaa](44007699.md) as president for the transitional period after he had served as the *[de facto](43594.md)* leader following the fall of the Assad regime.[^cite_note-english.enabbaladi.net-187]&#x20;

In March 2025, the UK-based [SOHR](34029669.md) reported that Syrian security forces and pro-government fighters had committed a [massacre of more than 1500 Alawite civilians](79404646.md) during [clashes in western Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2025_Western_Syria_clashes).[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189]&#x20;

On 10 March 2025, the SDF agreed to merge with the Syrian Armed Forces after SDF leader [Mazloum Abdi](59386296.md) met with al-Sharaa.[^cite_note-190] Three days later, al-Sharaa signed an [interim constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Interim_Constitution_of_Syria) covering a five-year transitional period.[^cite_note-191]&#x20;

On 29 March 2025, the [Syrian transitional government](79584988.md) was announced by Syrian president [Ahmed al-Sharaa](44007699.md) at a ceremony at the [Presidential Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Palace_of_Syria) in [Damascus](8914.md),[^cite_note-\:023-192] in which the new ministers were sworn in and delivered speeches outlining their agendas.[^cite_note-syria-tv-2025-03-29-193] The government replaced the [Syrian caretaker government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_caretaker_government), which was formed following the fall of the Assad regime on 8 December 2024.[^cite_note-auto3-194] Since then, a number of Western governments have lifted the sanctions on Syria, most notably the United States and the United Kingdom.[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196]&#x20;

The [2025 hunger crisis in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_hunger_crisis_in_Syria), considered the country's most severe modern humanitarian emergency, pushed over 14 million people into [food insecurity](216361.md#Syria), including 9.1 million facing acute hunger.[^cite_note-197] Driven by a 36-year [record drought](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Syria), economic collapse, and post-Assad instability, the crisis left over half the population in need of aid.[^cite_note-198] Between 6 and 11 January 2026, [fighting](82106178.md) in Aleppo between Syrian government forces and the Kurdish-led [Syrian Democratic Forces](48205248.md) had resulted in the deaths of 23 people and displaced 148,000 residents.[^cite_note-199] In October 2025, a [parliamentary election](80294606.md) was held under al-Sharaa's administration, the first since the fall of the Ba'athist regime.[^cite_note-200] The [People’s Assembly](4504009.md) held its inaugural session on 12 July 2026, after being delayed from 6 July. It was the first legislative session since the [fall of the Assad regime](78548066.md).[^cite_note-auto12-201][^cite_note-202][^cite_note-203]&#x20;

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Syria](27473.md)

Syria is the [twenty-fifth-largest country in Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Asian_countries_by_area). It borders [Turkey](11125639.md) to the north; [Iraq](7515928.md) to the east and southeast; [Jordan](7515964.md) to the south; [Israel](9282173.md) and [Lebanon](17771.md) to the southwest; and the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md) to the west. Syrian territory covers 185,180 km<sup>2</sup> (71,500 sq mi). Elevation ranges from the coastal plains of the Mediterranean to the mountains of western Syria, with [Mount Hermon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Hermon) being the country's highest point at 2,814 metres (9,232 feet). Much of central and eastern Syria consists of arid plateaus and desert regions, including parts of the [Syrian desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_desert), while the fertile valleys of the [Euphrates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euphrates), [Orontes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orontes_River), and [Khabur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khabur) rivers support agriculture and settlement. Significant natural resources include [petroleum](23195.md), natural gas, [phosphate rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phosphate_rock), asphalt, [marble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marble), [gypsum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gypsum), and arable land.&#x20;

### Climate



The Euphrates is the longest and one of the oldest rivers in [Western Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Asia)

Syria's climate varies from the humid Mediterranean coast, through a semi-arid steppe zone, to arid desert in the east. The country consists mostly of arid plateau, although the northwest part bordering the Mediterranean is fairly green. [Al-Jazira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Mesopotamia) in the northeast and [Hauran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hauran) in the south are important agricultural areas. The [Euphrates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euphrates), Syria's most important river, crosses the country in the east. Syria is one of the fifteen states that comprise the so-called "[cradle of civilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cradle_of_civilization)".[^cite_note-204] Its land straddles the northwest of the [Arabian plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_plate).[^cite_note-205]&#x20;

Petroleum in commercial quantities was first discovered in the northeast in 1956. The most important oil fields are those of al-Suwaydiyah, Karatchok, [Rmelan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rmelan) near [al-Hasakah](7573335.md), as well as [al-Omar](75129437.md) and al-Taym fields near [Deir ez-Zor](1429146.md). The fields are a natural extension of the Iraqi fields of [Mosul](200475.md) and [Kirkuk](199292.md). Petroleum became Syria's leading natural resource and chief export after 1974. Natural gas was discovered at the field of Jbessa in 1940.[^cite_note-USDOS-88]&#x20;

### Biodiversity

Main article: [Wildlife of Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_Syria)

See also: [Environmental issues in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_Syria)

Syria contains four terrestrial ecoregions: [Middle East steppe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_steppe), [Eastern Mediterranean conifer–sclerophyllous–broadleaf forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Mediterranean_conifer%E2%80%93sclerophyllous%E2%80%93broadleaf_forests), [Southern Anatolian montane conifer and deciduous forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Anatolian_montane_conifer_and_deciduous_forests), and [Mesopotamian shrub desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesopotamian_shrub_desert).[^cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-206] The country had a 2019 [Forest Landscape Integrity Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index) mean score of 3.64/10, ranking it 144th globally out of 172 countries.[^cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-207]&#x20;

## Politics and government

### Transitional period (2024–present)

#### Political system

Main articles: [Politics of Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Syria) and [Government of Syria](20933383.md)

Syria is currently undergoing a political transition following the [fall of the Assad regime](78548066.md) on 8 December 2024. It operates under a [unitary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state) [presidential system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_system) and, as of 2026, is governed by a [transitional government](79584988.md) during a [provisional period](598010.md).[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209] Currently, Syria operates as a [unitary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state) state, with the president serving as both head of state and head of government, although there are proposals to implement [decentralisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federalization_of_Syria).[^cite_note-210] Since the [Syrian Revolution Victory Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Revolution_Victory_Conference) banned the Ba'athist-era [National Progressive Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Progressive_Front_\(Syria\)), no new legislation regulating political parties has been enacted, and major political parties have yet to emerge in Syria.[^cite_note-211]&#x20;

#### Presidency and transitional government

See also: [Syrian caretaker government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_caretaker_government), [Syrian transitional government](79584988.md), and [Presidency of Ahmed al-Sharaa](82480307.md)



[Ahmed al-Sharaa](44007699.md)\
[President](516106.md)&#x20;



[Abdul Hamid al-Awak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul_Hamid_al-Awak)\
[Speaker of the People's Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speaker_of_the_People%27s_Assembly_of_Syria)



[Issam al-Khalif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Issam_al-Khalif)\
[President of the Supreme Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_presidents_of_the_Supreme_Constitutional_Court_\(Syria\))

After the [fall of the Assad regime](78548066.md), the presidency of Syria became *vacant* on 8 December 2024, with the duties of the head of state carried out by the [caretaker government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_caretaker_government), while [Ahmed al-Sharaa](44007699.md) served as the *[de facto](43594.md)* leader as commander of [Hayat Tahrir al-Sham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hayat_Tahrir_al-Sham) and head of the new Syrian administration. Outgoing Prime Minister [Mohammad Ghazi al-Jalali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Ghazi_al-Jalali) continued to serve in a caretaker capacity until [Mohammed al-Bashir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_al-Bashir) was designated prime minister by the Syrian General Command on 10 December 2024.[^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213]&#x20;

On 29 January 2025, during the [Syrian Revolution Victory Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Revolution_Victory_Conference) in Damascus, the Syrian General Command officially appointed al-Sharaa as president of Syria for the transitional period.[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215][^cite_note-defactoleader-216] In his first address as president on 31 January, al-Sharaa also announced plans to issue a "constitutional declaration" to serve as a legal reference following the repeal of the [Syrian Constitution of 2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Constitution_of_2012).[^cite_note-\:52-217] On 13 March 2025, al-Sharaa ratified the Constitutional Declaration, which established a five-year transitional period.[^cite_note-CNN20250313-218][^cite_note-WP20250313-219] On 29 March 2025, the caretaker government was replaced by the [Syrian transitional government](79584988.md), which was announced by al-Sharaa during a ceremony at the [People's Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Palace_\(Syria\)) in Damascus, and during which the new ministers were sworn in and delivered speeches outlining their agendas, while the position of prime minister was abolished.[^cite_note-\:1-220][^cite_note-\:02-221][^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223][^cite_note-\:22-224]&#x20;

#### Constitutional framework

See also: [Constitutional Declaration of the Syrian Arab Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Declaration_of_the_Syrian_Arab_Republic)



People's Assembly Building in [Damascus](8914.md), Syria, the seat of the Assembly.

Under the Constitutional Declaration, the powers of the [three branches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_powers) of government are distributed during the transitional period as follows: [legislative power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_power) is vested in the [People's Assembly](4504009.md); [executive power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_power) is exercised by the government under the leadership of the [president](516106.md); and [judicial power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judiciary) is vested in an independent judiciary, with judges subject only to the law.[^cite_note-newinterimconstition-225]&#x20;

Under the Constitutional Declaration, the President of Syria serves as the [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief) of the army and armed forces, appoints ministers, dismisses them from office, and accepts their resignations. The President also appoints one or more vice presidents, determines their powers, and may dismiss them or accept their resignations. If the presidency becomes vacant, a vice president assumes the President's powers. The position of vice president has remained vacant since the [fall of the Assad regime](78548066.md).[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-newinterimconstition2-227][^cite_note-newinterimconstition2-227] The president appoints one-third of the members of the People's Assembly, as well as the judges of the Constitutional Court, without requiring confirmation from the People's Assembly.[^cite_note-Ward-228]&#x20;

According to Article 39, the President promulgates laws approved by the People’s Assembly and has the right to object to them through a reasoned decision within one month of receiving them from the Assembly. A law subject to a presidential objection shall not be promulgated unless it is approved again by a two-thirds majority of the People’s Assembly, in which case the President must promulgate it by decree. Under Article 36, the President issues executive and regulatory regulations, control regulations, presidential orders, and decisions in accordance with the law.[^cite_note-newinterimconstition2-227][^cite_note-newinterimconstition2-227]&#x20;

Under Article 41, the President declares general [mobilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobilization) and war with the approval of the [National Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Council_\(Syria\)).[^cite_note-CNN202503132-229][^cite_note-WP202503132-230][^cite_note-newinterimconstition3-231]&#x20;

### Ba'athist Syria (1963–2024)

Main articles: [Ba'athist Syria](57134800.md), [Government of Ba'athist Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Ba%27athist_Syria), and [Politics of Ba'athist Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Ba%27athist_Syria)

The Syrian Arab Republic was a [presidential state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_republic)[^cite_note-232] that nominally permitted the candidacy of individuals who were not part of the Ba'ath-controlled [National Progressive Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Progressive_Front_\(Syria\)).[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234] Despite this, Syria remained a one-party state with an extensive [secret police](19204147.md) apparatus that curtailed any independent political activity.[^cite_note-auto1-235][^cite_note-236] The constitution introduced unilaterally by the Assad regime, without the participation of the [Syrian opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition), had bolstered its authoritarian character by bestowing extraordinary powers on the presidency, and a Ba'athist political committee continued to be responsible for authorization of political parties.[^cite_note-237]&#x20;



Former Syrian president [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md) with Ayatollah [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) in Tehran, Iran, 25 February 2019

The ruling Arab Socialist Ba'ath Party governed Syria as a [police state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_state) through its control of the [Syrian military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Arab_Armed_Forces) and security apparatus;[^cite_note-238] this system has frequently been described as [totalitarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Totalitarian),[^cite_note-tots-99] although some scholars have preferred the term [authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarian).[^cite_note-nt1-100][^cite_note-nt2-101][^cite_note-Heydemann,_Steven_19992-97] The 50th edition of [Freedom in the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_in_the_World), published by [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House) in 2023, designated Syria as "Worst of the Worst" among the "Not Free" countries and gave it the lowest score (1/100) alongside [South Sudan](32350676.md).[^cite_note-auto1-235][^cite_note-239]&#x20;

According to the [2012 Syrian constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Syria#Constitution_of_2012), the [President of Syria](516106.md) was the head of the Syrian state, while the [Prime Minister of Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Syria) was nominally the head of government,[^cite_note-con-2012-240] although real power in the system lay with the presidency.[^cite_note-241] The legislature, the [People's Assembly](4504009.md), was the body responsible for passing laws, approving government [appropriations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appropriation_\(law\)) and debating policy.[^cite_note-con-2012-240] In the event of a [vote of no confidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vote_of_no_confidence) by a simple majority, the prime minister was required to tender the resignation of their government to the president.[^cite_note-con-2012-240] Since the rule of Hafez al-Assad, the Ba'athist political system was centered around a comprehensive [cult of personality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Assad_family#Cult_of_Personality) focused on the Assad family;[^cite_note-242][^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245] with Alawite loyalists of the Ba'ath party dominating key positions in the military apparatus, secret police, and political establishment.[^cite_note-The_Sturdy_House_That_Assad_Built2-110]&#x20;

&#x20;The executive branch consisted of the president, two [vice presidents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_President_of_Syria), the prime minister, and the [Council of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Ministers_of_Syria) (cabinet). The constitution required the president to be a Muslim but did not make Islam the state religion.[^cite_note-USDOS-88][^cite_note-const-246] On 31 January 1973, Hafez al-Assad implemented a new constitution, which led to a national crisis. Unlike previous constitutions, this one did not require that the President of Syria be a Muslim, leading to fierce demonstrations in Hama, Homs, and Aleppo organized by the [Muslim Brotherhood](20742.md) and the traditional *[ulama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulama)*. They labelled Assad the "enemy of [Allah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allah)" and called for a *[jihad](16203.md)* against his rule.[^cite_note-247] The government survived a series of [armed revolts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamist_uprising_in_Syria) led mostly by [Islamists](15012.md) of the [Muslim Brotherhood](20742.md), between 1976 and 1982, through a series of repressions and massacres. The constitution gave the president the right to appoint ministers, to declare war and [state of emergency](298608.md), to issue laws (which, except in the case of emergency, require ratification by the People's Council), to declare amnesty, to amend the constitution, and to appoint civil servants and military personnel.[^cite_note-USDOS-88] According to the 2012 constitution, the president was elected by Syrian citizens in a direct election. Syria's legislative branch was the unicameral People's Council. The People's Council primarily served as an institution to validate Syria's one-party system and re-affirm the legislative proceedings of the Arab Socialist Ba'ath party.[^cite_note-248]



[Protest against the Assad regime](39750126.md) in the city of [Homs](77533.md), 3 February 2012

There was no independent judiciary in Syria, since all judges and prosecutors were required to be Ba'athist appointees.[^cite_note-auto1-235] Syria's judicial branches include the [Supreme Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Constitutional_Court_of_Syria), the [High Judicial Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Judicial_Council), the Court of Cassation, and the [State Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_agency) Courts. [Islamic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic) jurisprudence was a main source of legislation and Syria's judicial system had elements of [Ottoman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mecelle), [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_law), and [Islamic](28840.md) laws. Syria had three levels of courts: courts of first instance, courts of appeals, and the constitutional court, the highest [tribunal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribunal). Religious courts handled questions of personal and family law.[^cite_note-USDOS-88] The Supreme State Security Court was abolished by Bashar al-Assad in 2011.[^cite_note-decree53-249] As a result of the ongoing civil war, various alternative governments were formed, including the [Syrian Interim Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Interim_Government), the [Democratic Union Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Union_Party_\(Syria\)) and localized regions governed by sharia. Representatives of the Syrian Interim government were invited to take up Syria's seat at the Arab League in 2013 and[^cite_note-guardian.co.uk-250] was recognised as the "sole representative of the Syrian people" by several nations including the United States, United Kingdom, and France.[^cite_note-bbc.co.uk-251][^cite_note-bbc-252][^cite_note-usatoday.com-253]&#x20;

[Syria's elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Syria) were conducted through a [sham process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sham_election), characterised by wide-scale [rigging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rigging_election), repetitive voting and absence of [voter registration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voter_registration) and verification systems.[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255][^cite_note-auto2-256] Parliamentary elections were held on 13 April 2016 in the government-controlled areas of Syria, for all 250 seats of Syria's unicameral legislature, the Majlis al-Sha'ab, or the People's Council of Syria.[^cite_note-257] Even before results had been announced, several nations, including Germany, the United States and the United Kingdom, have declared their refusal to accept the results, largely citing it "not representing the will of the Syrian people."[^cite_note-258] However, representatives of the Russian Federation have voiced their support of this election's results. Various independent observers and international organizations have denounced the Assad regime's electoral conduct as a scam; with the United Nations condemning it as illegitimate elections with "no mandate".[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261][^cite_note-auto2-256] [Electoral Integrity Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_Integrity_Project)'s 2022 Global report designated Syrian elections as a "facade" with the worst electoral integrity in the world alongside [Comoros](5403.md) and [Central African Republic](5478.md).[^cite_note-262][^cite_note-263]&#x20;

### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Syria)

#### Ba'athist Syria



Syrian president [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md), Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md), and defence minister [Sergei Shoigu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergei_Shoigu) at [Khmeimim Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khmeimim_Air_Base), December 2017

Ensuring national security, increasing influence among its Arab neighbors, and securing the return of the [Golan Heights](7515865.md), have been the primary goals of Syria's foreign policy. At many points in its history, Syria has seen virulent tension with its geographically cultural neighbors, such as Turkey, Israel, Iraq, and Lebanon. Syria enjoyed an improvement in relations with several of the states in its region in the 21st century, prior to the Arab Spring and the Syrian civil war.&#x20;

Since the civil war of 2011 and associated killings and human rights abuses, Syria has been increasingly isolated from the countries in the region and the wider international community. Diplomatic relations have been severed with several countries including: Britain, Canada, France, Italy, Germany, Tunisia, Egypt, Libya, United States, Belgium, Spain, and the Arab states of the Persian Gulf.[^cite_note-264]&#x20;

From the Arab league, Syria continues to maintain diplomatic relations with Algeria, Egypt, Iraq, Lebanon, Sudan and Yemen. Following its violent suppression of the Arab Spring protests of the 2011 Syrian revolution, the Syrian government was suspended from the Arab League in November 2011 for over 11 years, until its reinstatement in 2023.[^cite_note-nytimes-suspend-133] Syria also quit the [Union for the Mediterranean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_for_the_Mediterranean).[^cite_note-265] After 11 years, the Arab League readmitted Syria.[^cite_note-266] The [Organisation of Islamic Cooperation](47725.md) suspended Syria in August 2012, citing "deep concern at the [massacres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_massacres_in_Syria) and inhuman acts" perpetrated by forces loyal to Bashar al-Assad.[^cite_note-CNN_OIC-152]&#x20;

#### Post-Ba'athist Syria



[Diplomatic relations of Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_of_Syria)

After the fall of the Assad regime and the subsequent formation of the Syrian transitional government, Syria has begun to re-establish diplomatic ties with nations that previously broke ties with the Ba'athist-led Syrian government during the [Syrian civil war](30741795.md), alongside the easing and removal of economic sanctions previously placed onto the Ba'athist government, including the [Caesar Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caesar_Syria_Civilian_Protection_Act) by the United States.&#x20;

The Syrian transitional government had also changed its stance on returning the Golan Heights to demand the [withdrawal of Israel](78549398.md) from areas beyond the border agreed upon in the 1974 Disengagement Agreement.&#x20;

The United States has also announced plans to re-open its embassy in Damascus, which was closed during the Syrian civil war on 21 February 2026, with the process approximately taking 15 days according to the [United States Department of State](31975.md).[^cite_note-267] Prior, [Tom Barrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Barrack), the United States special envoy to Syria, had risen the American flag at the ambassador's residence in May 2025.&#x20;

The Syrian transitional government had also restored diplomatic ties with [Turkey](11125639.md), with the latter now helping the former in the restructuring and training of the [Syrian Armed Forces](27479.md) alongside assisting the Syrian military during the [SDF–Syrian transitional government clashes](81146732.md) in the form of military aid.&#x20;

On 7 March 2026, following the lifting of Canadian economic sanctions on Syria, the [Bank of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Canada) agreed to open an account in the [Central Bank of Syria](9513014.md).[^cite_note-268]&#x20;

#### International disputes

See also: [Turkish occupation of northern Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_occupation_of_northern_Syria) and [Israeli occupation of the Golan Heights](60364960.md)

In 1939, while Syria was still a French mandate the French allowed a plebiscite regarding the [Sanjak of Alexandretta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanjak_of_Alexandretta) joining to Turkey as part of a treaty of friendship in World War II. In order to facilitate this, a faulty election was done in which ethnic Turks who were originally from the Sanjak but lived in [Adana](185243.md) and other areas near the border in Turkey came to vote in the elections, shifting the election in favor of secession. Through this, the [Hatay Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hatay_Province) of Turkey was formed. The move by the French was very controversial in Syria, and only five years later Syria became independent.[^cite_note-Morris9-269] Despite the Turkish annexation of the Sanjak of Alexandretta, the Syrian government has refused to recognize Turkish sovereignty over the region since Independence, except for a short period in 1949.[^cite_note-AlexandrettaTurkeyRelations-270]&#x20;



[Golan Heights](7515865.md) has been occupied by Israel since the [Six-Day War](29328.md).

The western two-thirds of Syria's Golan Heights region are since 1967 [occupied by Israel](2781576.md) and were in 1981 [effectively annexed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golan_Heights_Law) by [Israel](9282173.md),[^cite_note-occupiedSyrian-271][^cite_note-InternationalCommunityOccupiedTerritory-272] whereas the eastern third is controlled by Syria, with the [UNDOF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Disengagement_Observer_Force) maintaining a buffer zone in between, to implement the ceasefire of the [Purple Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purple_Line_\(ceasefire_line\)). Israel's 1981 Golan annexation law is not recognized in international law. The UN Security Council condemned it in Resolution 497 (1981) as "null and void and without international legal effect." Since then, General Assembly resolutions on "The Occupied Syrian Golan" reaffirm the illegality of Israeli occupation and annexation.[^cite_note-273] The Syrian government continues to demand the return of this territory.[^cite_note-274]&#x20;



Israeli-occupied [Golan Heights](7515865.md) and [Israeli invasion of Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Syria) in December 2024.

During [its invasion of Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Syria) in December 2024, Israel took control of the U.N.-patrolled buffer zone on Syrian territory, a move that violated the [1974 disengagement agreement](40148472.md) with Syria.[^cite_note-275] On 23 February 2025, Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) demanded the complete demilitarization of southern Syria in the provinces of [Quneitra](7445647.md), [Daraa](5317030.md) and [Sweida](3096933.md), and the withdrawal of Syrian forces from Syrian territory south of Damascus.[^cite_note-276] Israeli Defense Minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) said that Israeli forces would remain in southern Syria "for an indefinite period of time to protect our communities and thwart any threat."[^cite_note-277] Syria's new government under President [Ahmed al-Sharaa](44007699.md) condemned Israel's occupation of Syrian lands and demanded Israel's withdrawal.[^cite_note-278]&#x20;

The [Turkish Armed Forces](30205.md) and its ally the [Syrian National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_National_Army) have [occupied areas of northern Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_occupation_of_northern_Syria) since 2016, during the Syrian civil war.[^cite_note-279]&#x20;

In early 1976, Syria entered Lebanon, beginning their 29-year [military presence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_occupation_of_Lebanon). Syria entered on the invitation of Suleiman Franjieh, the Maronite Christian president at the time to help aid the Lebanese Christian militias against the Palestinian militias.[^cite_note-St._Petersburg_Times-280][^cite_note-281] Over the following 15 years of [Lebanese civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Civil_War), Syria fought for control over Lebanon. The Syrian military remained in Lebanon until 2005 in response to domestic and international pressure after the assassination of Lebanese Prime Minister [Rafik Hariri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafik_Hariri).[^cite_note-BBC-282]&#x20;

Another disputed territory is the [Shebaa farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shebaa_farms), located in the intersection of the [Lebanese-Syrian border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese-Syrian_border) and the Israeli-occupied Golan Heights. The farms, which are 11 km long and about 3 kilometers wide were occupied by Israel in 1981, along with rest of the Golan Heights.[^cite_note-283] Yet following Syrian army advances the Israeli occupation ended and Syria became the de facto ruling power over the farms. Yet after Israeli withdrawal from Lebanon in 2000, [Hezbollah](13919.md) claimed that the withdrawal was not complete because Shebaa was on Lebanese – not Syrian – territory.[^cite_note-284] After studying 81 maps, the United Nations concluded that there is no evidence of the abandoned farmlands being Lebanese.[^cite_note-285] Nevertheless, Lebanon has continued to claim ownership of the territory.&#x20;

### Military

See also: [Syrian Armed Forces](27479.md)



A Ba'athist [Syrian Army](3423927.md) soldier manning a checkpoint outside of [Damascus](8914.md) shortly after the outbreak of the [Syrian civil war](30741795.md), 2012

The President of Syria is commander in chief of the [Syrian Armed Forces](27479.md), comprising some 100,000 troops upon mobilization.[^cite_note-286] Until 2024, the military operated as a conscripted force, with males required to serve 30 months upon reaching the age of 18,[^cite_note-287] though had slowly decreased over time, from in two and a half years to two years in 2005, to 21 months in 2008 and to year and a half in 2011.[^cite_note-288] Since [late 2024](78548066.md), compulsory military service has been inactive and is no longer enforced.&#x20;

The breakup of the Soviet Union—long the principal source of training, material, and credit for the Syrian forces—may have slowed Syria's ability to acquire modern military equipment. It has an arsenal of surface-to-surface missiles. In the early 1990s, [Scud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scud)-C missiles with a 500-kilometre (310-mile) range were procured from North Korea, and Scud-D, with a range of up to 700 kilometres (430 miles), is allegedly being developed by Syria with the help of North Korea and Iran, according to Zisser.[^cite_note-289]&#x20;

Syria received significant financial aid from [Arab states of the Persian Gulf](3088555.md) as a result of its participation in the [Persian Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War), with a sizable portion of these funds earmarked for military spending. Iran and Russia were the biggest suppliers of military aid to the Assad-led Syrian Government.&#x20;

### Human rights

See also: [Human rights in Ba'athist Syria](78548501.md)



Wounded civilians arrive at a hospital in [Aleppo](159244.md), October 2012

Prior to the fall of the Assad regime, the situation for [human rights in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Syria) was a significant concern among independent organizations such as [Human Rights Watch](46530.md), who in 2010 referred to the country's record as "among the worst in the world."[^cite_note-290] The 2011 [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House) report[^cite_note-debate-291] ranked Syria "Not Free" in its annual [Freedom in the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_in_the_World) survey.[^cite_note-292] The Ba'ath regime was a totalitarian dictatorship that has been internationally condemned for its domestic and political repression, including [summary executions](36765422.md), [massive censorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_censorship_in_Syria),[^cite_note-hrw-2019-293][^cite_note-ohchr-2020-294] [forced disappearances](686148.md),[^cite_note-ReferenceA-295][^cite_note-ohchr.org-296] etc. as well as numerous crimes against Syrian civilians perpetrated during the civil war, such as massacres, [barrel-bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Syrian_civil_war_barrel_bomb_attacks), chemical attacks, etc.[^cite_note-un-chem-297][^cite_note-press.un.org-298]&#x20;

The authorities are accused of arresting democracy and human rights activists, censoring websites, detaining bloggers, and imposing travel bans. Arbitrary detention, torture, and disappearances are widespread.[^cite_note-ReferenceA-295][^cite_note-ohchr.org-296][^cite_note-hrw\.org-299] Although Syria's constitution guarantees gender equality, critics say that personal statutes laws and the penal code discriminate against women and girls. Moreover, it also grants leniency for so-called honour killing.[^cite_note-hrw\.org-299] As of 9 November 2011 during the uprising against President Bashar al-Assad, the United Nations reported that of the over 3,500 deaths, over 250 deaths were children as young as two years old, and that boys as young as 11 years old have been gang-raped by security services officers.[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301] [Syrian opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition_\(2011%E2%80%932024\)) forces claimed that more than 200, mostly civilians, were massacred and about 300 injured in Hama as a result of shelling by government forces on 12 July 2012.[^cite_note-302]&#x20;

In August 2013, the government was suspected of using chemical weapons against its civilians. U.S. Secretary of State [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) said it was "undeniable" that chemical weapons had been used in the country and that al-Assad's forces had committed a "moral obscenity" against his own people. "Make no mistake," Kerry said. "President Obama believes there must be accountability for those who would use the world's most heinous weapon against the world's most vulnerable people. Nothing today is more serious, and nothing is receiving more serious scrutiny".[^cite_note-303] The Emergency Law, effectively suspending most constitutional protections, was in effect from 1963 until 21 April 2011. It was justified by the government in the light of the continuing war with Israel over the Golan Heights.[^cite_note-USDOS-88][^cite_note-decree53-249]&#x20;

In August 2014, [UN Human Rights](22463.md) chief [Navi Pillay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navi_Pillay) criticized the international community over its "paralysis" in dealing with the civil war gripping the country, which by 2014 had resulted in 191,369 deaths with war crimes, according to Pillay, being committed with total impunity on all sides in the conflict. Minority Alawites and Christians were targeted by Islamists and other groups.[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305][^cite_note-un-chem-297][^cite_note-press.un.org-298] Three years later in April 2017, the U.S. Navy carried out a [missile attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Shayrat_missile_strike) against a Syrian air base[^cite_note-306] which had allegedly been used to conduct a [chemical weapons attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khan_Shaykhun_chemical_attack) on Syrian civilians, according to the U.S. government.[^cite_note-307] In November 2021, the U.S. Central Command called a 2019 airstrike that killed civilians in Syria "legitimate". The acknowledgement came after a New York Times investigation said the military had concealed the death of dozens of non-combatants.[^cite_note-308]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Syria](27476.md)

### Post-Ba'athist Syria

After the [fall of the Assad regime](78548066.md), the subsequent formation of the [Syrian transitional government](79584988.md) and the lifting of sanctions placed onto the Ba'athist-led government, Syria's economy has begun to see significant growth, with Syrian news agency Levant24 claiming that the country's GDP had risen to \$32 billion, experiencing a 30% to 35% growth in the GDP, alongside recording a budget surplus for the first time since the end of the Syrian civil war and expecting GDP to rise to \$60 or \$65 billion, around the same amount prior to the beginning of the civil war.&#x20;

Alongside this, recent visits by president [Ahmed al-Sharaa](44007699.md) had also opened opportunities for investments in the country, as seen with his meeting with the Syrian-British Business Council during his visit to the United Kingdom, alongside other meetings during his visit to Germany, with al-Sharaa seeking to strengthen economic ties with the latter.[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312]&#x20;

However, amidst the ongoing [2026 Iran war](82533582.md), food and other goods such as oil have also seen significant increases in their prices, exceeding 12% to 20% according to the [Syrian Arab News Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Arab_News_Agency), the state-owned news agency.[^cite_note-313]&#x20;

### Ba'athist Syria



Historical development of real GDP per capita in Syria since 1820



[Damascus](8914.md) before the war, 2008

The [2011 civil war](30741795.md) left a negative impact on Syria's economy. However, Syria's economy began declining even before the beginning of the war. Syria's share in global exports has eroded gradually since 2001.[^cite_note-wbgrowth10-314] The real per capita GDP growth was just 2.5% per year in the 2000–2008 period.[^cite_note-wbgrowth10-314] Poverty rates increased from 11% in 2004 to 12.3% in 2007.[^cite_note-wbgrowth10-314] In 2007, main exports included crude oil, refined products, raw cotton, clothing, fruits, and grains. The bulk of imports are raw materials essential for industry, vehicles, agricultural equipment, and heavy machinery. Earnings from oil exports as well as remittances from Syrian workers are the government's most important sources of foreign exchange.[^cite_note-USDOS-88]&#x20;



[Al-Hamidiyah Souq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Hamidiyah_Souq) in Damascus in 2010

Despite this, prior to the war, Syria was one of the most dynamic markets in the Middle East.[^cite_note-\:7-315] The government envisioned a transition of Syria into a free market economy.[^cite_note-\:7-315] The rising oil reserves triggered rapid growth in the economy, creating business opportunities for foreign firms and investments, as the booming oil and gas industry and infrastructure projects made Syria an attractive market.[^cite_note-\:7-315] Prior to the war the government hoped to attract new investment in the tourism, natural gas, and service sectors to diversify its economy and reduce its dependence on oil and agriculture.[^cite_note-\:7-315] The government began to institute economic reforms aimed at liberalizing most markets, but those reforms were slow and ad hoc, and were completely reversed since the outbreak of conflict.[^cite_note-316] The [UNDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNDP) announced in 2005 that 30% of the population lived in poverty, and 11.4% live below the subsistence level.[^cite_note-USDOS-88] At the outset of the civil war, Syria was classified by the [World Bank](36752.md) as a "lower middle income country,"[^cite_note-data.worldbank.org-317] though in 2010 Syria remained dependent on the oil and [agriculture sectors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_sector).[^cite_note-wbbrief-318] The oil sector provided about 40% of export earnings.[^cite_note-wbbrief-318]&#x20;

During the civil war, the [Syrian economy](27476.md) relied upon dwindling customs and income taxes which are heavily bolstered by lines of credit from Iran,[^cite_note-revenues_shrink-319] Russia and China.[^cite_note-economycrumble-320] Iran is believed to have spent between \$6 billion and US\$20 billion per year on Syria during the first four years of the civil war.[^cite_note-321] The [Syrian pound](456708.md) lost 80% of its value, with the economy becoming part [state-owned](204768.md) and part [war economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_economy).[^cite_note-322] Proven [offshore](9969688.md) expeditions have indicated that large sums of oil exist on the Mediterranean Sea floor between Syria and Cyprus.[^cite_note-323] The agriculture sector contributes to about 20% of GDP and 20% of employment. Oil reserves are expected to decrease in the coming years, and Syria has become a net oil importer.[^cite_note-wbbrief-318] As of 2012, the value of overall exports was slashed by two-thirds, from the figure of US\$12 billion in 2010 to only US\$4 billion in 2012.[^cite_note-AFP_economy-324]&#x20;

&#x20;Since 2012, oil and tourism industries in particular have been devastated, with \$5 billion lost.[^cite_note-AFP_economy-324] Sanctions have sapped the government's finances. U.S. and European Union bans on oil imports, which went into effect in 2012, are estimated to cost Syria about \$400 million per month.[^cite_note-shortap-325] Around 40% of all employees in the tourism sector lost their jobs since the beginning of the war.[^cite_note-economyhang-326] In May 2015, [ISIS](9087364.md) captured Syria's phosphate mines, one of the Syrian government's last chief sources of income.[^cite_note-327] The following month, ISIS blew up a gas pipeline to Damascus that was used to generate heating and electricity in Damascus and Homs; "the name of its game for now is denial of key resources to the regime" an analyst stated.[^cite_note-328] In addition, ISIS was closing in on [Shaer gas field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaer_gas_field) and three other facilities in the area—Hayan, Jihar and Ebla—with the loss of these western gas fields having the potential to cause Iran to further subsidize the Syrian government.[^cite_note-329] Reconstruction will cost as much as \$10 billion.[^cite_note-AFP_economy-324] [Aleppo soap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleppo_soap) is a popular product of Syria.



&#x20;A cove in [Latakia](195594.md) in 2014

The economy was highly regulated by the government, which increased subsidies and tightened trade controls to assuage protesters and protect [foreign currency reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_currency_reserve).[^cite_note-CIA-3] Long-run economic constraints include foreign trade barriers, declining oil production, high unemployment, rising budget deficits, and increasing pressure on water supplies caused by heavy use in agriculture, rapid population growth, industrial expansion, and water pollution.[^cite_note-CIA-3] In 2024, the World Bank estimated that the Syrian GDP had contracted by 84% from 2010 to 2023. As of 2023, its GDP was \$6.2 billion. Syria's new government has vowed to stamp out the production of captagon, which makes up a major portion of the informal economy. The new government will also face challenges in securing and resuming the production of oil, as rebuilding the industry will require significant investments with no guarantee of a return.[^cite_note-330]&#x20;

#### Drug industry

Main article: [Ba'athist Syrian Captagon industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ba%27athist_Syrian_Captagon_industry)

Prior to the fall of the Ba'athist regime, Syria was home to a burgeoning [illegal drugs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prohibition_of_drugs) industry run by associates and relatives of Bashar al-Assad.[^cite_note-\:0-331] It mainly produced [fenethylline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fenethylline), or known as captagon, an addictive [amphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphetamine) popular in the Arab world. As of 2021, the export of illegal drugs eclipsed the country's legal exports, leading the *New York Times* to call Syria "the world's newest [narcostate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narco-state)".[^cite_note-\:0-331] The drug exports allow the government to generate hard currency and bypass [international sanctions](67328508.md).[^cite_note-\:0-331][^cite_note-332][^cite_note-auto-333] Captagon was Syria's primary export, valued at a minimum of US\$3.4 billion annually, surpassing the country's largest legal export, olive oil, which is valued at around US\$122 million per year.[^cite_note-334]&#x20;

### Energy

This section is an excerpt from [Energy in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Syria).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Energy_in_Syria\&action=edit)]



Pumpjack

[Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_energy) in Syria is mostly based on [oil](23195.md) and [gas](22131.md).[^cite_note-Energy_in_Syria_\:1-335] Some energy infrastructure was damaged by the [Syrian civil war](30741795.md). There is high reliance on [fossil fuels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel) for energy in Syria,[^cite_note-336] and [electricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electricity_in_Syria) demand is projected to increase by 2030, especially for industry activity such as [automation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automation).[^cite_note-337] However, conflict in Syria has caused electricity generation to decrease by nearly 40% in recent years due to plant destruction and fuel shortages.[^cite_note-338] Electricity access in daily life for Syrians has also been altered due to conflict. Electricity to residents of Syria is largely provided by private [diesel generators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diesel_generator), which is costly and limited in hours of use.[^cite_note-Energy_in_Syria_\:13-339] Conflict has increased household electricity expenditures while also decreasing [household income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disposable_household_and_per_capita_income).[^cite_note-Energy_in_Syria_\:13-339] Some households have since turned to [solar energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_Energy_Generating_Systems) as a supplementary source of energy, though high costs limit widespread adoption.[^cite_note-Energy_in_Syria_\:13-339]&#x20;

### Agriculture

This section is an excerpt from [Agriculture in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Syria).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Agriculture_in_Syria\&action=edit)]

Agriculture is a key part of the [economy of Syria](27476.md). The sector still accounts for an estimated 26 percent of gross domestic product (GDP) and represents a critical safety net for the 6.7 million Syrians – including those internally displaced – who still remain in rural areas. However, agriculture and the livelihoods that depend on it have suffered massive losses. Today, food production is at a record low and around half the population remaining in Syria are unable to meet their daily food needs.[^cite_note-340]&#x20;

Until the mid-1970s, [agriculture in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Syria) was the primary [economic activity in Syria](27476.md). At independence in 1946, [agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture) (including minor [forestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forestry) and [fishing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishing)) was the most important sector of the economy, and in the 1940s and early 1950s, agriculture was the fastest growing sector. Wealthy merchants from urban centers such as Aleppo invested in [land development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_development) and irrigation. The rapid expansion of the cultivated area and increased output stimulated the rest of the economy. However, by the late 1950s, there was little land left that could easily be brought under cultivation. During the 1960s, [agricultural output](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_productivity) stagnated because of political instability and [land reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_reform). Between 1953 and 1976, agriculture's contribution to [GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GDP) increased (in constant prices) by only 3.2%, approximately the rate of population growth. From 1976 to 1984 growth in agriculture declined to 2% a year, and its importance in the economy declined as other sectors grew more rapidly.&#x20;

In 1981, as in the 1970s, 53% of the population was still classified as [rural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rural), although movement to the cities continued to accelerate. However, in contrast to the 1970s, when 50% of the [labor force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_force) was employed in agriculture, by 1983 agriculture employed only 30% of the labor force. Furthermore, by the mid-1980s, unprocessed farm products accounted for only 4% of exports, equivalent to 7% of non-petroleum exports. Industry, commerce, and transportation still depended on [farm produce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Produce) and related [agro-business](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agro-business), but agriculture's preeminent position had clearly eroded. By 1985 agriculture (including a little forestry and fishing) contributed only 16.5% to GDP, down from 22.1% in 1976.&#x20;

By the mid-1980s, the [Syrian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_government) had taken measures to revitalize agriculture. The 1985 investment budget saw a sharp rise in allocations for agriculture, including [land reclamation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_reclamation) and [irrigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irrigation). The government's renewed commitment to agricultural development in the 1980s, by expanding cultivation and extending irrigation, promised brighter prospects for Syrian agriculture in the 1990s.&#x20;

During the [Syrian Civil War](30741795.md), the agricultural sector has witnessed a drop in producing all kinds of commodities such as wheat, cotton and olives,[^cite_note-341] due to the lack of security and immigration of agricultural workforce,[^cite_note-342] especially in [Al-Hasakah Governorate](1429110.md) and [Aleppo Governorate](7444618.md).&#x20;

### Transport

Main articles: [Transport in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Syria) and [Rail transport in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Syria)



Expressway M5 near [Al-Rastan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Rastan), [Homs](7445154.md)

Syria has four international airports ([Damascus](4202280.md), [Aleppo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleppo_International_Airport), [Latakia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latakia_International_Airport) and [Qamishli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qamishli_International_Airport)), which serve as hubs for [Syrian Air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Air) and [Fly Cham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fly_Cham) and are also served by a variety of foreign carriers.[^cite_note-343]&#x20;

Syria has 2,850 kilometres (1,770 mi) of [railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Railways), out of which 1,500 kilometres (930 mi) are out of operation in 2025 because of damage sustained during the civil war. The [rolling stock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_stock) is also in a state of disrepair.[^cite_note-DailySabah-05212025-344] As of 2024 there are no international rail services, but [high-speed rail in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-speed_rail_in_Turkey) is being extended close to the border.[^cite_note-345] The last train between Syria and Jordan operated in September 2011, and the transitional government plans to rebuild the Syrian section of the [Hejaz Jordan Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Jordan#Hedjaz_Jordan_Railway).[^cite_note-346] The first trial run of a passenger train between Aleppo and Hama took place in August 2025 after months of renovation work, with plans to restore regular service between the two cities.[^cite_note-347]&#x20;

The road network in Syria is 69,873 kilometres (43,417 miles) long, including 1,103 kilometres (685 miles) of expressways. The country also has 900 kilometres (560 miles) of navigable but not economically significant waterways.[^cite_note-CIA-3]&#x20;

### Internet and telecommunications

[Telecommunications in Syria](27477.md) are overseen by the [Ministry of Communications and Information Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Communications_and_Information_Technology_\(Syria\)).[^cite_note-348] In addition, [Syrian Telecom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Telecom) plays an integral role in the distribution of internet access.[^cite_note-349]&#x20;

During the [Syrian civil war](30741795.md), the [Syrian Electronic Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Electronic_Army), served as a Ba'athist-backed pro-[Assad regime](57134800.md) group in cyberspace, and according to [Vice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_\(magazine\)), were supposedly an enemy of the decentralized [hacktivist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hacktivism) collective [Anonymous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anonymous_\(hacker_group\)).[^cite_note-350] Under Ba'athist rule, 13,000 internet activists were arrested in 2011 and 2012.[^cite_note-351]&#x20;

### Water supply and sanitation

Main article: [Water supply and sanitation in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Syria)

Syria is a semiarid country with scarce water resources. The largest water consuming sector in Syria is agriculture. Domestic water use stands at only about 9% of total water use.[^cite_note-Agriculture-352] A big challenge for Syria before the civil war was its high population growth (in 2006 the growth rate was 2.7%[^cite_note-353]), leading to rapidly increasing demand for urban and industrial water.[^cite_note-World_Bank-354]&#x20;

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Syria) and [List of cities in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Syria)

| Year | Pop. | ±% p.a. |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1960 | 4,565,000 | — |
| 1970 | 6,305,000 | +3.28% |
| 1981 | 9,046,000 | +3.34% |
| 1994 | 13,782,000 | +3.29% |
| 2004 | 17,921,000 | +2.66% |
| 2011 | 21,124,000 | +2.38% |

| 2019 estimate[^cite_note-355] 2024 estimate [^cite_note-356] Source: [Syrian Planning and Statistics Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Planning_and_Statistics_Commission) of the Syrian Arab Republic, 2011[^cite_note-cbspop-357] |  |  |

Most people live in the [Euphrates River valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euphrates) and along the coastal plain, a fertile strip between the coastal mountains and the desert. Overall population density before the civil war was about 99 per square kilometre (258 per square mile).[^cite_note-358] According to the *World Refugee Survey 2008*, published by the U.S. Committee for Refugees and Immigrants, Syria hosted a population of refugees and asylum seekers numbering approximately 1,852,300. The vast majority of this population was from Iraq (1,300,000), but sizeable populations from [Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_\(region\)) (543,400) and [Somalia](27358.md) (5,200) also lived in the country.[^cite_note-World_Refugee_Survey_2008-359]&#x20;

In what the UN has described as "the biggest humanitarian emergency of our era",[^cite_note-360] by 2014 about 9.5 million Syrians, half the population, had been displaced since March 2011;[^cite_note-Reuters_Half-361] 4 million were outside the country as refugees.[^cite_note-UNHCR-146] By 2020, the UN estimated that over 5.5 million Syrians were living as refugees in the region, and 6.1 million others were internally displaced.[^cite_note-The_Refugee_Brief_2020-362]&#x20;
